# Daily Job Scout — Progress Log (spine)

**Candidate:** Maleeha Bilal — CV at `assets/FDE-CV/Maleeha-Bilal-CV-Forward-Deployed-Engineer-A4.pdf`. One CV, no variants: the Forward-Deployed-Engineer and Software-Engineer positionings were merged and the US-Letter copies removed on 2026-08-04, so there is exactly one document to cite and it cannot drift.
**Target role:** Forward Deployed Engineer / Solutions Engineer / client-facing delivery engineer — plus junior full-stack (TypeScript/React/Next.js) as the fallback lane.
**Search lanes:** (A) Pakistan + remote via the Indeed PK connector · (B) international FDE track — Palantir, Databricks, Google Cloud (PSO/Customer Engineering), Salesforce, and comparable delivery-engineering employers.
**Visa:** requires sponsorship. Postings silent on sponsorship are **still shortlisted**, flagged `sponsorship: unverified`. **The CV no longer says so and that changes nothing here.** The header clause "Requires visa sponsorship" was removed on 2026-08-04 — it was the third line a screener read, and work authorisation is asked on every application form anyway, so declaring it up front only invited a filter before anyone reached the projects. She still requires sponsorship; that fact lives on this line, not on the CV. Do **not** read the CV's silence as `sponsorship: not needed`. The header still names **UK / EU / Gulf** relocation targets, which is what standing decision 12 rests on.
**Purpose:** every job ever surfaced is logged here so it is never shown twice. Read this file at the START of every beat; append at the END of every beat.

It is not a disqualifier and it is not something to hide or explain away in a rationale — just expect it to surface in screening.

**Pending evidence (update this line when it lands):** a flagship web app built specifically to demonstrate FDE capability. Not shipped yet. Once live, log the URL and repo here and re-open every posting marked `reopen-when-flagship-ships`.

## Standing decisions

1. **Judge against the fact sheet above, not against a stale one.** The 2026-08-01 beat ran on an out-of-date profile that recorded zero programming experience. That profile is void. Everything logged that day is eligible for re-evaluation.
2. **Match on demonstrated delivery, not on years.** "2+ years" on a posting whose actual requirements are React/TypeScript/deployment is a shortlist with the gap flagged. "3+ years professional software development" plus a stack she has never touched is a drop.
3. **Pull the full JD before judging.** Titles lie in both directions — "Intern" can require a year of AWS; "Engineer, Deployment Strategy" can be entry-friendly.
4. **A keyword hit is not a match.** State which fact-sheet line the posting maps to. If the rationale needs a hedge ("may be considered", "or equivalent"), it is not a match.
5. **Sponsorship is a flag, not a filter.** Log `sponsorship: yes / no / unverified`. Never claim it is available unless the posting says so.
6. **CS-degree-required is a drop** unless the posting says "or equivalent practical experience" *and* the stack matches.
7. **Never invent experience.** No rationale may imply a job she has not held.
8. **Scarcity is not fit.** If nothing qualifies, return an empty list and say so plainly.
9. **Cap output at 3–5 jobs per beat.** Best fits only.
10. **Dedup on title + company, never on job ID alone.** `JOBSEARCH_*` numbers are assigned per
    search session, not per posting — the same Taraz role was `JOBSEARCH_1` on 08-01 and
    `JOBSEARCH_14` on 08-03. Treating the ID as stable makes dedup fail silently and re-shows
    everything. Log the ID anyway (it is the handle for `get_job_details` within a run), but match
    against the log on title + company. Lane B postings fetched from a career page keep their URL as
    the stable key. Found by beat 2, which proposed it as PR #1; landed by hand, since the routine
    may propose rule changes but never merges them.
11. **A posting that states no requirements has not stated a low bar — it has stated nothing.**
    Silence is not evidence, and a shortlist may not rest on the *absence* of a disqualifier. Name the
    fact-sheet line the posting affirmatively asks for; if the only argument available is "there is no
    gap to substitute for", that is an argument from silence and the posting is a drop. Where a JD is
    all hedges ("maybe you founded a company"), rule 4 already forbids it. Corollary: pay band and
    seniority language are requirements even when no years figure is given. Found by beat 4's checker
    on a £120–160k London FDE posting that named no degree, no years and no stack.
12. **Search UK and the Gulf by city, not by "remote".** `forward deployed engineer` against remote/GB
    returns 0 across phrasings; the same intent against `London` returns the live UK FDE market. The
    CV names **UK / EU / Gulf** as relocation targets, so Dubai/Abu Dhabi and Riyadh belong in the
    standing rotation — one Dubai query on beat 4 returned Salesforce, BCG X (including an
    internship-grade FDE post) and two ElevenLabs deployment roles, the densest FDE-titled yield of
    any single query in four beats.
13. **A qualifier governs only the clause it attaches to.** "Preferred", "ideally", "such as" and
    "or similar" narrow the phrase they sit inside; they do not soften the whole bullet. Palantir's
    require list reads "**Strong engineering background**, preferred in fields such as Computer
    Science, Mathematics, Software Engineering, Physics, and Data Science" — the requirement is a
    strong engineering background, and what is *preferred* is which field it sits in. Beat 5's draft
    read "preferred" as governing the bullet, which turned a requirement she fails into an optional
    nice-to-have, and the checker caught it. Corollary, from the same beat: **the verb decides.**
    Two near-identical Abu Dhabi "deployed engineer" postings split on two word pairs — Gecko's
    "*or relevant experience*" and "*familiarity with* data structures" (kept) against Brain Co.'s
    "*or a related field*" and "*strong foundation in* ... data structures and algorithms" (dropped).
    Read these postings clause by clause; skimming for stack overlap gets both directions wrong.
14. **Where a market is searched matters as much as how, and a title search can report an empty
    country that is not empty.** Standing decision 12 established that the connector needs a city
    rather than "remote". Beat 6 found the next level down: `forward deployed engineer solutions
    engineer` against **Berlin** returns 0, while a bare `n8n` query against Berlin returns 10 —
    the FDE vocabulary has not travelled to the German market, so Germany must be searched **by
    employer or product name**, not by title. Germany then earns a standing slot on evidence
    stronger than coverage. Every Lane B keep and near-miss in six beats has been
    `sponsorship: unverified`; n8n's postings are the first to state the answer — *"We can sponsor
    visas to Germany; for any other country, you need to have existing right to work"* — which
    simultaneously makes its London req an affirmative `sponsorship: no` and makes Germany-based
    reqs at European product companies the highest-yield geography in the rotation for a candidate
    who requires sponsorship. **Corollary, and the reason this is a rule rather than a note:** a
    single sentence in a benefits block can decide a whole geography, so read the sponsorship line
    on every Lane B posting even when the requirements have already dropped it — the drop is about
    the posting, the sponsorship line is about the employer, and the second outlives the first.
15. **A rare keyword is not a shortlist engine.** Beat 6's draft put up two borderlines and both
    rested on n8n, which appears on the fact sheet and had shown up on roughly one posting in the
    previous five beats combined — then on five of the eight judged in one run. In every case the
    qualifier around it parked it in the optional tier ("would be highly beneficial", "is a massive
    plus", under "Nice-to-haves") or bound it to a named gap ("proficient in Python, **and** n8n,
    and API architecture"). The checker's finding: *"assembling a shortlist because the rare keyword
    finally showed up is the Palantir tell — 'the lowest years bar ever seen at a Lane B target
    employer' — wearing a different costume."* Rule 8 covers scarcity of **matches**, not only
    scarcity of jobs. Corollary from the same beat: **a doubt stated in the draft's own frictions
    section about a *graded* clause is not a friction to disclose, it is the verdict** — conceding
    the failing clause and shortlisting anyway is the beat-4 Linux Recruit pattern.

## Verdict vocabulary

`shortlisted` · `skipped` · `dropped by checker` · `already applied` · `reopen-when-flagship-ships` · `re-evaluate` · `expired`

`re-evaluate` means **seen but never judged** — the JD could not be pulled, so standing decision 3 was not satisfied. It is not a soft skip: a `re-evaluate` row is a debt the next beat must clear before anything else, and it is the one verdict that title + company dedup must not skip. Used on beat 4 when the connector rate-limited mid-run (JOBSEARCH_224).

`expired` means **the posting left the index before it could be judged** — searched for across several phrasings over two or more beats and not returned, while the employer is demonstrably still listing. It closes a `re-evaluate` debt without pretending a verdict was reached: it is a fact about the posting, not a judgement on the candidate, and it is the only permitted exit from `re-evaluate` other than a real verdict. A row may only be marked `expired` after **two** beats of failed retrieval, and the failed queries must be named in the row. Proposed on beat 5 for JOBSEARCH_224 (Fun Prime Technology), which has now missed two beats — held at `re-evaluate` pending this rule, since inventing the verdict to clear the queue is exactly what the debt rule forbids.

**An `expired` row does not suppress a re-listing.** It records that a posting left the index — not that anyone read it — so it is the second verdict title + company dedup must not skip. If that title and company come back to the results, it is a new posting: pull the JD and judge it. Deduping against an expired row would bury a posting nobody has ever judged, which is the exact failure `re-evaluate` exists to prevent, arriving through the exit instead of the entrance.

Drafted on MB Dev Studio's requirements→scoping→build→deploy line matching "You find the problem > You design the solution > You build it", and on "you already spend your spare time experimenting with agents, AI tooling" matching MCP / agent orchestration / n8n. Checker dropped it and named the flaw precisely: the draft's load-bearing move was *an argument from silence* — "there is no gap to substitute for" — and you cannot verify a candidate against skills a posting does not name. The one gradeable clause, "You'll likely have experience in high-growth environments, startup ecosystems or highly demanding engineering cultures", she fails on all three branches, and the draft conceded it in its own frictions section before shortlisting anyway. The rest of the JD is hedges ("**Maybe** you founded a company"), which rule 4 forbids as a basis. £120–160k in London prices a senior hire, and through a recruiter the real client bar is only ever higher than the copy. `sponsorship: unverified`. |

And "expert user of AI Coding Assistants" ≠ MCP: exposing tooling *to* agents is not demonstrated Copilot/Claude Code use in a professional workflow, and neither appears on either CV. Posted 19 Feb 2026 (~5.5 months stale). |

Beat 5 proposed closing it "if it does not return by beat 7", but the rule that actually landed in the verdict vocabulary requires **two** beats of failed retrieval, which beat 6 completes; the stricter of the two conditions is the one written down. Everything about it is right: a named Lane B target employer, a Gulf city her CV names as a relocation target, an FDSE role whose description ("work directly with customers to quickly understand their greatest problems and design and implement solutions") reads like her MB Dev Studio line, and a **require list of only three bullets** carrying the lowest years bar ever seen at a Lane B employer — "**1+ years** of relevant, post-college work experience", not 3+. Drafted as an explicit borderline on bullet three, "strong coder with shown proficiency in ... **TypeScript/JavaScript**, or similar", which is a clean affirmative match to the Next.js/TypeScript/Vercel site and OpenClaw Prep. Checker dropped it on the other two bullets, both failing on stated text: (1) 1+ years against ~3 months of MB Dev Studio, which is also her own venture rather than an employer relationship, and counting the banking or teaching years as *relevant* is the AdalFi breach; (2) "**Strong engineering background**, preferred in fields such as Computer Science, Mathematics, Software Engineering, Physics, and Data Science" — the draft had neutralised this by reading "preferred" as governing the whole bullet, and **it governs only the field list**: the requirement is a strong engineering background, what is preferred is which field it sits in. Against "no CS/SE degree" and "no paid employment as a software engineer, ever" she fails the required half and is merely un-preferred on the optional half — and Palantir states the bullet twice, in *What We Value* and again in *What We Require*. Checker also named the draft's own tell: its load-bearing sentence was "the lowest years bar ever seen at a Lane B target employer", which is a scarcity argument, rule 8. "Wrangling massive-scale data" collides with the production-at-scale gap. Posted 27 Feb 2026 (~5 months stale). `sponsorship: unverified`. |

Rule 6's first half therefore opens, and its second half needs the stack to match, which it does inside the **graded** bullets rather than the company blurb: "Proficiency with programming languages such as Java, C++, Python, **JavaScript**, or similar" and "familiarity with ... **front-end frameworks**" map to *"six-section consultancy site on Next.js App Router + TypeScript + Tailwind, deployed on Vercel"*, *"OpenClaw Prep — React Native / Expo / TypeScript"* and the deployed HTML/JS/Tailwind MCQ app; "familiarity with ... **cloud infrastructure**" maps to *"n8n on Oracle Cloud ARM64 via Docker, Tailscale Funnel for HTTPS without a public IP, plus a seven-phase runbook another engineer reproduced unaided"*. React is named in *Technologies We Use*. Checker tested the AWS/GCP defence hard and upheld it: both appear only under *Technologies We Use*, a description of the company's own stack, while the graded bullet says "cloud infrastructure" generically — the distinction is the Huron precedent in reverse, where "Python, and AWS or Azure" sat **inside** the requirements. Checker also confirmed the keep does not rest on silence (rule 11): there are eight named gradeable bullets and she meets or partially meets each, so the absence of a years figure and a pay band is not evidence and does not appear in the rationale. Frictions disclosed, not papered over: no paid engineering employment ever, and MB Dev Studio is ~3 months old and her own venture; **AWS and Google Cloud are the company's named technologies and both are stated gaps** — she would learn them on the job; "familiarity with data structures, storage systems" she clears at *familiarity* level (Firestore, offline-first sync) with no algorithms preparation on record, an interview risk rather than a JD fail; **"ability to work effectively in teams of technical and non-technical individuals" is a partial, not a match** — the fact sheet says no team-of-engineers experience and she has never mentored engineers, and requirements elicitation must not be presented as team experience; the domain is industrial/defence robotics (US Navy, Air Force, boiler inspections) and nothing on the fact sheet touches it; travel up to 50%, office-first culture, Abu Dhabi relocation. `sponsorship: unverified` — but note affirmatively that **this JD states no Arabic requirement**, unlike the Salesforce Dubai FDE that died on it. | 2026-08-09 | JOBSEARCH_808 | Full Stack Software Engineer | UNiDAYS | London (hybrid, EC1Y 1AA) | Dropped by checker | **The only draft of the run, forwarded as an explicit borderline, and the checker dropped it as over-determined on rules 15, 11, 4 and 13.** JD pulled; the requirements block was quoted verbatim and every one of its 11 bullets marked met/unmet before drafting (beat 8's corrective, working as designed). Score: 4 met by shipped artifact (TypeScript · "experience with modern React" · "an understanding of modern web frameworks, **including Next.js**" · the e2e half of the testing bullet, via the CV's Playwright harness), 2 met soft, **1 unmet outright**, 3 part-unmet, 1 neutral. **The drop turns on the bullets the draft itself conceded.** (a) "An understanding of **agile methodologies** such as scrum, kanban etc." — rule 13, the "such as" governs *which* methodologies, so the requirement is unqualified and has zero fact-sheet coverage; rule 15's corollary makes a conceded graded clause the verdict, not a friction. (b) "Support with ensuring **high availability of our critical systems** and being proactively involved in **monitoring and incidents**" against the fact-sheet line *no production system at scale, no on-call* — the beat-8 `_625` ruling verbatim: a fact-sheet negative in direct contradiction with a duty bullet is the verdict, not a doubt to disclose. (c) "Knowledge on web best practices for performance, accessibility, core web vitals and SEO" — four-item conjunctive, one covered (programmatic SEO); the checker cited the log's own precedents, `JOBSEARCH_38` (AL GHANI, kept) where Core Web Vitals was only a *screening extra* and still forced into the frictions, and `JOBSEARCH_102` (Simpaisa, skipped) where it was a named drop reason — so this posting's position is worse than either. (d) All four artifact-backed METs are **frontend** on a posting titled **Full Stack** whose headline mission is "the continuing transition of our identity product … powered by our new **distributed, event-driven, cloud-based architecture**", against the fact-sheet line *Node.js is a skills-line keyword with no shipped standalone Node backend* — beat 7's defect in frontend clothes. (e) The draft's closing support ("no years figure, no degree gate, title is neither junior nor senior") is three facts about what the posting does **not** say — rule 11, and confessing it in the draft is not an exemption. (f) On level, the checker overturned the draft's neutral score using rule 13's verb test: "you **practise** writing maintainable code, **reviewing code, pair programming** at times" is present-tense assertion of existing habit, not bullet 7's "a desire to"; add "betterment of **other engineers in the company**" and "work closely with **engineering leaders**" and three separate clauses land on the stated gap *no team-of-engineers experience*. **Worth carrying forward:** this is only the **second posting in nine beats to name Next.js inside its requirements block** — the first was AL GHANI, the log's cleanest keep. The stack seam is genuine; what killed this one was the four non-stack bullets (agile, accessibility/CWV, team practice, on-call). Screen for those four *before* drafting next time. Sponsorship: unverified (silent). |

We'll support you with visa assistance, travel costs, and your first month of accommodation."** That is the **first affirmative employer-level sponsorship statement in nine beats** — n8n London, CLERA, Cognizant, SCOR and Aristocrat all state the negative — and it names two relocation targets the CV lists (UK, UAE) plus three EU hubs. Rule 5 runs one way only: sponsorship stops a posting being dropped for the wrong reason, it never rescues one. **Revolut goes on the standing watch list as a sponsoring employer, with a recurring query for non-degree-gated reqs.** Sponsorship: yes. |

The payments domain — the one place her Askari Bank line would live — appears only under "Experience in one or more of the below is a **plus**", so rule 15's corollary applies: the graded clause she meets is optional, the required ones she fails. The one required bullet she partially meets is "Experience in operating systems (**Linux** and MS Windows)", omitted from the draft and recorded here for completeness. Independently stale: **posted 02 March 2026**, five months old, which this log treats as its own factor (ContentStudio at ~9 months, Arete at 26 Feb). Sponsorship: unverified. |
| 2026-08-11 | JOBSEARCH_1056 | Data Insights & Automation Engineer | OptimaGov | Remote (USA only) | Skipped | JD pulled, and the **only posting in the run with no degree requirement and no years bar stated anywhere** — the rarest shape in eleven beats. Dropped on three independent grounds. (1) "*Location: Remote (**USA ONLY**)*" is a hard geographic gate for a candidate in Islamabad who requires sponsorship. (2) "Experience with **SQL and relational databases**" is a required bullet and a named gap — the fact sheet's only datastore is Firebase/Firestore, and beat 10 already flagged "a datastore beyond Firestore" as a repeating cause of near-misses. (3) "**Strong** Python programming experience" against a fact sheet that says Python is real but lighter than TypeScript with one shipped artifact (the Playwright/MCP harness) — the ContentStudio pattern. Recorded because the pull is genuinely tempting in the other direction: the *preferred* list reads "Background in Data Science, Analytics, Statistics, **Mathematics**" and "financial analytics, **accounting, auditing, budgeting**", which her MSc Economics & Finance (Distinction, 3.92) and the dated Askari Bank audit/reconciliation/financial-statement work hit almost line for line. Rescuing a posting on its preferred list while conceding a required one is exactly what rule 15's corollary forbids, and the JD self-labels the block "Preferred Qualifications (**not required**)". The checker flagged one omission from the draft, and it is the fit-*favourable* line — the responsibility bullet "**Support the onboarding and configuration of new customer environments**", the single most FDE-shaped sentence in the posting. It is recorded here because leaving out the favourable line makes a drop look easier than it was; it does not change the verdict, since the geographic gate is dispositive before any skills analysis (the Rune Seattle ground, beat 7, and the IBM ground, beat 8). Rule 13's "the verb decides" corollary supplies the Python citation: "**Strong** Python programming experience" is the Brain Co. "strong foundation in" form, not Gecko's "familiarity with". Sponsorship: no (US-only). |
| 2026-08-11 | JOBSEARCH_945 | Technical Support Engineer / Technical Support Technician | Rayyanco Business Systems | Karachi / Sialkot / Gujrat / Mirpur / Gujranwala / Islamabad | Skipped | JD pulled. Sent to the checker as the run's explicit borderline, because it is the **only posting of 54 new ones with no CS-degree gate and an explicit fresh-graduate welcome** — "Qualification: Graduate or Diploma holder"; "Minimum 1 year of relevant experience **preferred**"; "**Fresh graduates are also encouraged to apply**" — and it sits in **Banking Automation** ("build your career in Banking Automation", "Support Banking Automation solutions"). **The draft dropped it on rule 11 and the checker corrected the reasoning while upholding the drop — the correction is the point of this row.** Rule 11 is the wrong hook: it bars a shortlist resting on the *absence* of a disqualifier, and this posting *does* state affirmative requirements — the seven-item Required Skills list (communication, customer focus, empathy, active listening, patience, professionalism, time management) — essentially all of which she meets on IELTS 7.0, the 2023–2026 teaching post, and MB Dev Studio's "requirements → scoping → build → deploy → post-launch support". There is no argument from silence here. The drop stands on four other grounds. **Rule 4:** the only *technical* requirement stated is troubleshooting "Banking Automation solutions", which no fact-sheet line covers — and the Askari Bank line is banking *employment*, not implementing banking software (the checker's own AdalFi finding, 2026-08-03). **Scope:** a six-city in-person field-support technician seat (Travel Expense, Mobile recharge) is in neither the FDE/solutions/delivery lane nor the junior full-stack lane — same ground as `_19` IT Support Analyst L2, `_62` IT Support Engineer and `_21`/`_23` NOC Engineer. **Rule 11's *corollary* does apply:** "Technician" in the title and the Rs35,000–80,000 band are grade language, and grade language is a requirement even when no years figure is given. **Rule 8:** keeping the one posting with no degree gate *because* it is the one posting with no degree gate is scarcity standing in for fit. Islamabad is listed last of six cities. Sponsorship: n/a (domestic). |
| 2026-08-11 | JOBSEARCH_952 | Technical Account Manager - Strategy | Motive | Lahore | Skipped | JD pulled. The degree line **does** carry equivalence — "Technical degree in Computer Science or Engineering preferred, **or equivalent practical experience**" — so rule 6 opens under standing decision 13, and rule 6's second half then closes it. "1 to 2 years of experience in Technical Support **with a permanent employment status**" is a rule-2 drop on employment she has never held, and the phrase plus "**Deep familiarity with Motive's product suite**, specifically focusing on Compliance, Asset, and Safety" reads as an internal-mobility req. Also "Create **SQL-based queries**", "hardware fault analysis, firmware upgrades", telematics/TMS integrations, on-call rotation, and "**mentoring** Technical Support Engineers within your segment" — which collides with the fact sheet's stated no-team-of-engineers gap — as the on-call rotation collides with the stated no-on-call gap. The controlling precedent on the equivalence clause is ContentStudio (beat 2's checker): *"'or equivalent experience' clears the first half of rule 6 but not the second — the stack must match too."* It does not here. Two checker additions. The draft omitted the **US Export Administration Regulations** authorisation clause ("The applicant must be authorized to receive and access those commodities and technologies controlled under U.S. Export Administration Regulations"), which belongs in the log under standing decision 14's corollary — read the authorisation line even when the requirements have already dropped the posting. And this is a **domestic Lahore req at a US product company**, i.e. one of the very few postings in eleven beats where sponsorship is a non-issue: **Motive Pakistan earns a watch-list slot for a junior rung**, the way Revolut earned one for sponsorship. Sponsorship: n/a (domestic). |
| 2026-08-11 | JOBSEARCH_1013 | Senior Product Engineer (TS/NodeJS/Vue) | n8n | Berlin | Skipped | **A re-surface, not a new posting** — the checker caught the dedup miss: this exact title + company was judged on 2026-08-06 as `JOBSEARCH_506`, **located in London**, where n8n's sponsorship line makes it an affirmative `sponsorship: no`. Standing decision 10 dedups on title + company, so it should not have been re-pulled. The row is kept rather than folded into the re-surface list for one reason: **the location differs and the sponsorship consequence flips with it.** n8n's own location disclaimer explains why — "If you see multiple job postings for the same role, it is most likely because we're hiring remotely for this role and posting in different locations". The Berlin listing of the same req carries "**We can sponsor visas to Germany; for any other country, you need to have existing right to work.** Our company language is English", making it `sponsorship: yes` where the London listing of the same req is `sponsorship: no`. That is a fact about the **employer's geography**, not the posting, and standing decision 14's corollary says the second outlives the first. JD pulled. Senior, and a clean drop on the fact sheet's two most explicit gaps: "You've **built or operated high-traffic SaaS systems**" against "no production system at scale, no on-call, no team-of-engineers experience", and "Develop scalable **Node.js services and APIs**" against "Node.js is a skills-line keyword with no shipped standalone Node backend". Pulled anyway, and logged, for standing decision 14's corollary — read the sponsorship line on every Lane B posting even when the requirements have already dropped it. It re-states verbatim: "**We can sponsor visas to Germany; for any other country, you need to have existing right to work.** Our company language is English." Sponsorship: **yes** (Germany). That is the third n8n req to carry the clause, and it holds the Germany geography open even though this particular rung is out of reach — rule 5 runs one way only and sponsorship never rescues a posting. |
| 2026-08-11 | (WebSearch, no Indeed ID) | AI Forward Deployed Engineer (Early Career) | Salesforce | Remote, within the United States | Skipped | Found by WebSearch, not on the Indeed index — `Salesforce AI Forward Deployed Engineer Early Career` / remote US returns **0**, confirmed by the checker independently. `builtin.com` is **egress-blocked** from the cloud (same class as the Palantir/Databricks 403s, IBM careers and openai.com), so the JD could not be pulled directly. **The draft's `Skipped` verdict survives but its reasoning was rebuilt by the checker, and the rebuild matters.** The `Skipped`-not-`Re-evaluate` precedent (IBM on beat 8, OpenAI London on beat 9) turns on one test: the drop must not rest on the *unread* requirements block. IBM was dropped on **location**; OpenAI on years floors quoted verbatim from two independent listings. The draft's grounds were paraphrased snippets — exactly the case the precedent excludes. The verdict holds on two better grounds instead. **(1) Location:** listings place it as remote *within the United States* — the IBM ground exactly, and a non-requirements ground. **(2) An omitted and decisive clause** the draft's summary left out: "**Strong background in Computer Science or a related engineering discipline**" — the "or a related field" form (Workato / ARFA / BCG X pattern), so rule 6 never opens. | 2026-08-12 | JOBSEARCH_1087 · JOBSEARCH_1213 | Implementation & Support Specialist – AI | CareCloud | Karachi (in person) | Dropped by checker | **The run's only draft keep, and the most expensive correct drop since beat 4.** JD pulled (twice — the checker's re-run returned it as `_1213` in the same session, a fresh confirmation of standing decision 10). Distinct from CareCloud's existing "React Js Developer" rows (`_82`/`_140`), so dedup did not cover it. Drafted because the shape is the target role and the usual gates are absent: post-sale client ownership, onboarding calls, requirements gathering, QA before handover, issue triage — with **no years bar anywhere**, and none of SQL, Windows Server or Excel, the three unqualified bullets that killed Contour on beat 11. Five of six required bullets map to fact-sheet lines. **Dropped on the sixth: "Background in Computer Science or a related technical field (does not need to be a software engineering background)."** The checker's ruling, which is the finding of the beat: this is **not** the same clause as beat 11's Salesforce line — no "Strong", "technical field" is broader than "engineering discipline", and the parenthetical genuinely widens — **but softer is not open.** Rule 6 opens on a specific verbal form, "or equivalent practical experience", and this JD does not contain it; standing decision 13's beat-5 split is the exact test, Gecko's "*or relevant experience*" (kept) against Brain Co.'s "*or a related field*" (dropped), and "or a related **technical field**" sits on the dropped side one word away from the form that would have opened it. The controlling precedent is not Salesforce but **Red Star Technologies (`_942`, judged 2026-08-11)**, dropped on the identical trailing phrase — overturning this would mean overturning a beat-11 row. **What the parenthetical actually does:** it widens the sub-field, it does not substitute practical experience for the field. The three clauses the draft leaned on — "does not need to be a software engineering background", "even without deep coding experience", "Not a software engineering role" — all relieve the *coding* bar while leaving the *field* bar standing, so **they cut against her, not for her**: the posting is expressly not buying shipped code, which is her only asset in this clause. Her field is Economics/Finance/Education and the PIAIC Agentic AI credential is incomplete (expected May 2027). **Rule 15's corollary is dispositive:** the draft filed this clause under "frictions" and shortlisted anyway — the beat-4 Linux Recruit pattern verbatim, and the conceded clause is the verdict. Two further checker corrections, both fit-**favourable** and both omitted by the draft: the Progree line "*Ran network reconnaissance and traffic analysis with **nmap and Wireshark***" is the best evidence on the CV for the "read logs/error messages" bullet, better than the three things the draft cited; and the 2007–2009 Askari line ("audit and reconciliation procedures, daily deposits/advances/account-holder reporting, fixed-asset registers") is far stronger for "track multiple client accounts, open issues, and follow-ups simultaneously" than "MB Dev Studio client engagements", which names zero clients. Pay is "**From** Rs100,000.00 per month" — a floor, which the draft's quotation dropped. Sponsorship: n/a (domestic). |
| 2026-08-12 | (WebSearch, no Indeed ID) | Forward Deployed Engineer - UAE | OpenAI | Abu Dhabi (hybrid, 3 days/week; travel up to 50%) | Re-evaluate | **Seen but never judged — a real debt, and beat 13's first work.** The draft logged this `Skipped`; **the checker overturned it to `Re-evaluate`**, and the reasoning is worth keeping because it closes a loophole. The `Skipped`-not-`Re-evaluate` precedent permits exactly two routes and neither is available here. **(a) A non-requirements ground** — unavailable: Abu Dhabi sits inside the CV's "Open to relocation (UK / EU / Gulf)", the role is hybrid rather than US-remote, so neither the IBM (beat 8) nor the Salesforce (beat 11) location ground applies, and no Arabic gate surfaced. **(b) A years floor quoted verbatim from independent listings** (the beat-9 OpenAI-London route) — unavailable, and the checker *proved* it rather than asserting it: two WebSearches this run returned two **mutually inconsistent** renderings of the same bullet, "5+ years of engineering experience and proficiency in developing production-grade code" against "5+ years of engineering or technical deployment experience, ideally in customer-facing environments". Two different strings for one bullet is conclusive evidence that neither is verbatim — both are summaries of a page nobody fetched, which is beat 11's Salesforce case exactly. Beat 9's verbatim London floor **cannot be transferred**: that finding is scoped "in the UK", and beat 11's checker already rejected this precise import once. **The IBM "unclearable debt" objection was tested and fails:** IBM's ground was a factual claim about this environment, and it is not true here — `forward deployed engineer solutions engineer` and `forward deployed engineer` against Abu Dhabi both return a live FDE index this session (Mistral, Palantir ×2, Gecko, BRAIN ×4); OpenAI is simply absent from it today. A clearable debt is not a permanent one, and the vocabulary already has the exit: `expired`, after two beats of named failed retrieval. Treating "the page is blocked" as itself sufficient for `Skipped` would make the precedent vacuous — every egress-blocked posting would qualify. **Blocked domains, named so the debt is auditable:** openai.com, jobs.ashbyhq.com, fwddeploy.com, ycombinator.com, remotedxb.com, fde.academy, paraform.com, ae.expertini.com. No fetchable mirror exists. **Beat 13 must re-query Abu Dhabi for OpenAI by name and record the phrasings.** Sponsorship: unverified. **— BEAT 13 UPDATE (2026-08-13): still `Re-evaluate`, and this is reported rather than cleared.** The debt was worked as the beat's first work and neither exit opened. **Indeed, five phrasings, all recorded:** `OpenAI` / Abu Dhabi → 10 results, **no OpenAI**; `forward deployed engineer` / Abu Dhabi → 8 (Mistral, Palantir ×2, Gecko, BRAIN ×4), **no OpenAI**; `forward deployed engineer solutions engineer` / Abu Dhabi → 7, **no OpenAI**; `OpenAI forward deployed engineer` / Abu Dhabi → **0**; `OpenAI forward deployed` / Dubai → 1 (NTT DATA), **no OpenAI**. OpenAI has never once appeared on the Indeed index in thirteen beats. **Newly blocked hosts this beat, named so the debt stays auditable:** `simplify.jobs` and `uae.el7far.com`, joining openai.com, jobs.ashbyhq.com, fwddeploy.com, ycombinator.com, remotedxb.com, fde.academy, paraform.com and ae.expertini.com — **the checker confirmed openai.com, jobs.ashbyhq.com and fwddeploy.com blocked at first hand rather than taking the draft's word for it**. Still no fetchable mirror. **The finding that settles (a), and it belongs to the checker: the 5+ years floor is a search-priming artefact, not a corroboration.** The draft argued the floor was now established because one exact string had reproduced across two beats. The checker ran three sweeps and varied the *query* instead of the phrasing: a sweep containing **"years of engineering"** returned *"the search results don't contain specific information about a minimum number of years"*; a neutral sweep asking for the **requirements list** also returned no floor; and only the sweep whose query itself contained **"5+ years"** returned the floor. **The one sweep that produced the number is the one that supplied it.** Verbatim reproduction under a primed query is not independent reproduction, the count of distinct renderings has risen from two to **three**, and beat 12's ruling is strengthened rather than weakened. Beat 9's verbatim London floor still cannot be transferred (scoped "in the UK"; beat 11 rejected the import once already). **`Expired` is unavailable and the checker confirmed the draft's reading:** `expired` means the posting *left the index*, and this posting demonstrably has not — `openai.com/careers/forward-deployed-engineer-uae-abu-dhabi-uae/` returned live in all three sweeps today. Marking it `Expired` would assert a false fact about the posting, and the vocabulary says `expired` is a fact about the posting. `Skipped` is unavailable too: no non-requirements ground exists (Abu Dhabi is inside the CV's UK/EU/Gulf line, the role is hybrid not US-remote, no Arabic gate), and two sweeps record "**OpenAI offers relocation assistance**", which cuts *toward* viability. Checker phrasings, recorded: `OpenAI "Forward Deployed Engineer" Abu Dhabi UAE qualifications "years of engineering"`, `"Forward Deployed Engineer" OpenAI Abu Dhabi "5+ years"`, `OpenAI Forward Deployed Engineer UAE Abu Dhabi role requirements list`. **A verdict-vocabulary change is proposed by PR this beat** (a third exit, `unreachable`, for a posting provably live and provably out of reach) — proposed, not merged, and the row is **not** downgraded in the meantime. Sponsorship: unverified; "relocation assistance" is not a sponsorship statement. |

| 2026-08-13 | JOBSEARCH_1256 (checker's re-pull: `_1344`) | Partner Implementation Specialist I | TCP Software (TimeClock Plus) | Lahore | **Shortlisted** | **Delivered. The first keep to survive the checker since beat 5, and it survived on affirmative matches rather than on absences.** Found by the beat-11 varied-phrasing action applied to a city that phrasing had never been run against — `implementation specialist customer onboarding software` / **Lahore**. Zero prior "TCP Software" rows. JD pulled by the draft and **re-pulled independently by the checker**, which confirmed every quotation verbatim and confirmed standing decision 10 again inside one beat (`_1256` → `_1344`). **The combination that has not appeared in thirteen beats: an open experience band and no field-of-degree gate at the same time.** Beat 12's lane finding 2 concluded "the bar that blocks her domestically is not experience and has not been for several beats — it is the field of the degree"; CureMD, AIO App and Eposmatic each cleared the first and failed the second. This posting clears both. **Rule 6 opens twice over:** "Bachelor's degree in MIS **or equivalent work experience** preferred" — the "or equivalent" form is the Gecko side of beat 5's split (kept) against Brain Co.'s "or a related field" (dropped), i.e. the one verbal form beat 12's "softer is not open" ruling identifies as actually opening rule 6 — and the whole bullet is then graded "preferred" under standing decision 13. Rule 6's second half (the ContentStudio precedent, "the stack must match too") is vacuous here because **the Requirements block names no technology at all**; the only product named is "1–2 core TCP products", which the same bullet says she will *learn*. **That is what separates it from the two keeps that died:** Contour (`_1086`, beat 11) fell on three unqualified Required bullets naming Windows Server, strong Excel and CSV transformation; CareCloud (`_1087`, beat 12) fell on the field-of-degree form. Neither failure mode is present. **The five affirmative matches, each to a CV line:** "Strong written and verbal communication skills" → "stakeholder communication, runbooks and technical documentation", IELTS 7.0 full professional proficiency, Teacher 2023–2026, and Kay & A's "**client-facing administrative support**"; "Basic understanding of software platforms and data configuration" → "Node.js, REST APIs, Firebase (Auth, Firestore), **JSON Schema**" and "Docker, Vercel, Oracle Cloud (ARM64), Linux, Tailscale, CI/CD"; "Support customer and partner training through standard **virtual** sessions" → "Economics & Business Teacher... Designed curriculum and taught quantitative problem solving" (and the Progree internship is itself logged "Virtual"); "Maintain accurate project records and documentation" → "wrote a **seven-phase runbook that let another engineer reproduce it unaided**", plus Askari's "monthly-to-annual financial statements" and "fixed-asset registers with depreciation"; "Perform basic system configuration and setup **with guidance**" → "Deployed an n8n automation stack on ARM64 Linux with Docker, fronted by Tailscale Funnel for stable HTTPS without a public IP". **Rationale corrected by the checker on the load-bearing bullet, and the correction matters.** The draft mapped "**0–1 years** of experience in a customer support or technical onboarding role" to MB Dev Studio's "post-launch support". The checker struck it: a founder-led studio three months old naming **zero clients** is not "a customer support or technical onboarding role", and asserting it edges toward rule 7. **The bullet is satisfied on its face because the stated band includes zero** — beat 12 already recorded CureMD's "0–1 year" and AIO App's "0–2 years" as genuinely open bars — and the honest adjacent evidence is Kay & A (Oct 2006 – Feb 2007), which is dated, employed and client-facing. The stretch was unnecessary and is deleted. **Second checker addition, fit-favourable and omitted by the draft: the product domain is payroll.** TCP sells "timekeeping, scheduling, and **leave management**" and this seat handles "**PEO** client accounts" — a PEO is a payroll-outsourcing structure — against Askari's "**payroll and tax deductions**". Not a required bullet and not dressed as one, but real, dated and on the CV. **Seniority language points down, not up** (rule 11's corollary, inverted): compensation field is empty, and the body reads "Perform basic system configuration and setup **with guidance**", "**Escalate** complex integration or partner-specific issues **to senior team members**", "**Build foundational knowledge**", "**Assist in** implementing". **Frictions disclosed, neither a gate.** (1) **Lahore, not Islamabad** — the fact sheet says "a Lahore posting is a **relocation call**, not a commute", assigning the decision to the candidate; the checker read the posting end to end and confirmed **no onsite requirement, no residency clause, no shift clause**, the direct contrast being Podium below, which states both. Honest caveat the checker added: the CV header offers relocation to **UK / EU / Gulf** only, so domestic relocation is not advertised on the document itself. (2) Rule 15's corollary was tested and does not bite — location is not a graded clause of this JD, it is not a clause of this JD at all. Sponsorship: **no** (stated) — "we are not able to sponsor visas for this role" — with no practical effect on a domestic seat for a Pakistan-based candidate, and **scoped "for this role"**, so it is not employer-wide intelligence under standing decision 14's corollary. |
| 2026-08-13 | JOBSEARCH_1399 | AI Full-Stack & Automation Engineer | COLOR STUDIO PROFESSIONAL | Korangi, Karachi (in person) | reopen-when-flagship-ships | **The densest artifact-level match in the log, and it fails on exactly one thing.** Found on a Lane B axis re-run against a Lane A city (`technical implementation engineer customer deployment` / Karachi), an axis pairing never tried before. New employer, posted 2026-08-08, Rs100,000–150,000. **No degree requirement anywhere, no years bar anywhere**, and hiring is portfolio-first — the application asks for "GitHub profile, Portfolio, Links to live products you personally built, Links to any published iOS/Android applications... How you currently use **Claude Code**, Cursor, ChatGPT or other coding agents". **The met half, line for line against shipped artifacts:** the mobile block names "React Native / Expo / TypeScript / Authentication / **Subscriptions** / **In-app purchases** / **Google Play deployment**" against OpenClaw Prep, which is React Native · Expo (EAS) · TypeScript · Firebase Auth · offline-first sync · **in-app purchases** · "Release build in closed testing on **Google Play Console**"; automation names "**Playwright**, Browser automation, ... **n8n**", both shipped; AI-assisted development names "**MCP tools**" and Claude Code, and MCP is a fact-sheet line with an artifact behind it; cloud names "**Vercel** ... **Docker**, GitHub, GitHub Actions, **CI/CD**", all on the skills line. **It drops on the JD's explicit "What We Are NOT Looking For" block** — "**Cannot design a backend independently**", "**Cannot design a database**", "Has only frontend experience" — against fact-sheet line 38 (Node.js is a skills-line keyword with **no shipped standalone Node backend**) and the datastore position (Firestore is the only datastore on record; no PostgreSQL, no SQL). **The checker's ruling, and it sets a precedent the log did not carry: an explicit "What We Are NOT Looking For" list is the *strongest* requirement form in a JD, binding harder than a Required bullet** — a Required bullet states what the employer wants, an exclusion bullet states the filter used to reject, and standing decision 13's "the verb decides" test finds no softening verb at all in "Cannot design a backend independently". Rule 15's corollary is then dispositive: the draft conceded the clause and shortlisted anyway, which is the beat-4 Linux Recruit pattern for the third consecutive beat. **On whether the density of the met half changes it, the checker said no, and located why:** the met items sit in the Mobile, Automation, AI-assisted-development and Cloud blocks, while the Full-Stack, Backend & Data and AI blocks name Node.js, PostgreSQL, SQL, relational schemas, queues, data pipelines, caching, search, "**OpenAI APIs, Anthropic / Claude APIs**", embeddings and vector search — a near-complete enumeration of the fact sheet's named gaps — plus a Shopify ecosystem absent from the CV entirely. "This is not 'mostly met with two gaps'; it is two halves and she owns one." **Logged `reopen-when-flagship-ships` rather than `Skipped`, on the checker's recommendation, and it is the first time that verdict has been clearly earned rather than decorative:** everything failing is one thing — a backend with a relational schema — and the fact sheet's pending-evidence line exists for exactly this. **Reopen condition: the flagship ships a Node/Postgres backend.** Also "Work Location: In person", Korangi. Sponsorship: n/a (domestic). |
| 2026-08-13 | JOBSEARCH_1232 (checker's re-pull: `_1398`) | Deployment Strategist | Palantir Technologies | Abu Dhabi | Skipped | **A new title + company pair at a core Lane B target — the log carried Palantir *FDSE* rows only, so standing decision 10 did not cover it and it had never been judged.** JD pulled by the draft and re-pulled by the checker. Its entire "What We Value" block: "Extraordinary ability to take on open-ended problems in unstructured environments" · "Adaptive and introspective; willing to learn, teach, lead and follow" · "Ability to travel 25 – 75% **required**" · "Experience with programming, scripting or statistical packages (e.g. Python, R, Matlab, SQL) **is a plus**". **No degree requirement, no years bar, no required stack** — materially different from the Palantir FDSE clause ("Strong engineering background, preferred in fields such as Computer Science...") that founded standing decision 13. **Verdict upheld, ground overturned, and the ruling is the finding of the beat.** The draft dropped it on rule 11 (argument from silence, the Linux Recruit `_193` pattern). **The checker refused that ground and ruled that responsibilities blocks *are* affirmative asks under rule 11:** the rule says "name the fact-sheet line **the posting** affirmatively asks for" — *the posting*, not the requirements block — and standing decision 3 exists precisely because the whole JD is the evidence. Matching a fact-sheet line to a responsibility is an affirmative argument; absence is the only thing rule 11 forbids. **But responsibilities defeat rule 11's main clause only and cannot carry a keep** — they answer "is there anything affirmative here at all?", not rule 4 or rule 11's corollary. That is the beat-11 Rayyanco move exactly: refuse rule 11 as the ground, then rebuild. **The rebuilt ground is rule 4 and lane, and the JD draws the line itself:** "Identify relevant datasets... and **work with Forward Deployed Engineers** to integrate the data into a stable and extensible pipeline" — the posting places the FDE as a different person on the same team, and the target role is a client-facing *delivery engineer*. **The sharp version, which is beat 12's CareCloud finding inverted: a seat that grades programming "is a plus" is a seat where her single differentiated asset is worth the least.** The clause reads as widening and in fact narrows the contest to the other three bullets — one partial match ("willing to learn, **teach**, lead and follow" → Teacher 2023–2026), one working-conditions clause, and one unevidenced trait assertion ("**Extraordinary** ability..."), the same intensifier shape as the "**extensive** experience" clause beat 12 rebuilt the Linux Recruit drop on. **The checker explicitly declined the draft's alternative ground "Palantir's known hiring bar" — that is reputation, not the JD, and standing decision 3 forbids it.** *Fit-favourable and genuinely strong rather than decorative:* "**Lead training sessions** to ensure that the product is meeting user needs"; "**Present the results of our work**... to audiences ranging from analysts to **C-suite executives**"; "Go onsite and meet with customer analysts to **understand the critical questions they need to answer** and locate their biggest pain points"; "the desire to **dive into the details of the data**" — against MSc Economics & Finance (Distinction, CGPA 3.92) and Askari's audit/reconciliation/financial-statement line. **Employer intelligence: Palantir's Deployment Strategist ladder is the least-gated Palantir req in the log — no degree clause at all — and the one Palantir ladder where her non-engineering half is the asset rather than the liability.** Watch list, Okta-style. Sponsorship: unverified (checker read the full posting; note also "you must work from the city and or country in which you are employed"). |

Riyadh is inside the CV's Gulf target, **no Arabic gate appears**, and "Relevant certifications (e.g., CISSP, CompTIA Security+, CEH) **are a plus**" — graded, so their absence is not a ground. - **JOBSEARCH_193 Linux Recruit — DROPPED.** The finding is the sharpest methodological catch of any beat so far: the draft's load-bearing move was *an argument from silence* — "there is no degree gate, no years figure and no named stack, so there is no gap to substitute for" — and the checker's line is **"you cannot verify a candidate against skills a posting does not name."** A JD with no stated requirements is not a JD with no bar; it is a JD whose bar is unseen, and rule 3's "titles lie in both directions" extends to the *absence* of requirements. The one gradeable clause — "you'll likely have experience in high-growth environments, startup ecosystems or highly demanding engineering cultures" — she fails on all three branches, and the draft had conceded it in its own frictions section before shortlisting anyway. Everything else in the JD is a hedge ("**Maybe** you founded a company"), which rule 4 forbids as a basis.
- **JOBSEARCH_194 Decoded — DROPPED.** "You are **an experienced software engineer or developer**" against "no paid employment as a software engineer, ever". Checker: this is the Mehdi Technologies finding run in reverse — *"there the drafter silently read the word software **into** a clause; here the drafter silently reads experienced **out** of one."* It also holed the double-fit the draft was built on: the JD's named deliverable is re-skilling professional developers **into Java/C#**, both named gaps, and *"teaching economics is not training software engineers, the same way banking employment is not building banking software."* And "expert user of AI Coding Assistants" ≠ MCP — exposing tooling *to* agents is not demonstrated Copilot/Claude Code use inside a professional developer workflow.

**Fact-sheet discrepancy found by the checker (proposed by PR, not pushed here).** The draft rationale for JOBSEARCH_193 cited her "banking background" as domain literacy for wealth-management clients. The checker verified this against both CV variants in `assets/FDE-CV/` and found that **the banking experience appears on neither CV**: both carry exactly three experience entries — MB Dev Studio, Progree, Lahore School System — with banking present only inside the summary phrase "15 years in education and finance". The fact sheet's line "banking accounts/audit/payroll, financial statement preparation" therefore describes history that a screener reading the CV cannot see. This matters in both directions: rationales must stop leaning on it as if it were visible, and if it is real it belongs on the CV. This is the AdalFi precedent one step further on — there, banking employment was wrongly treated as equivalent to building banking software; here it was cited from a document that does not contain it.

The checker's finding is a reading error worth keeping: the draft neutralised "**Strong engineering background**, preferred in fields such as Computer Science, Mathematics, Software Engineering, Physics, and Data Science" by taking "preferred" to govern the whole bullet, and **it governs only the field list** — the requirement is a strong engineering background, what is preferred is which field it sits in. Against "no CS/SE degree" and "no paid employment as a software engineer, ever" she fails the required half and is merely un-preferred on the optional half; Palantir states the bullet twice, in *What We Value* and again in *What We Require*. Bullet one fails independently: 1+ years of *relevant* post-college work experience against ~3 months of MB Dev Studio, which is also her own venture, and counting banking or teaching as relevant is the AdalFi breach. Checker also named the draft's tell — its load-bearing sentence was "the lowest years bar ever seen at a Lane B target employer", which is rule 8, scarcity.
- **JOBSEARCH_332 Gecko Robotics — KEPT.** "Every graded clause in *About You* is either affirmatively met by a shipped artifact or is pitched at *familiarity*, and the degree line is the genuine article." **"or relevant experience"** is practical equivalence, not the degree-*field* clause that killed ARFA, BCG X, BRAIN and Salesforce, so rule 6 opens; its second half is met inside the graded bullets (JavaScript proficiency, front-end frameworks, cloud infrastructure) rather than in the company blurb. The checker tested the AWS/GCP defence hard and upheld it — both appear only under *Technologies We Use*, describing the company's stack, while the graded bullet says "cloud infrastructure" generically; the Huron drop was correct precisely because "Python, and AWS or Azure" sat **inside** its requirements. It also confirmed the keep does not rest on silence (rule 11): eight named gradeable bullets, met or partially met, so the missing years figure and pay band are not evidence and stay out of the rationale. One mapping was **downgraded from match to partial**: "teams of technical and non-technical individuals" — the fact sheet says no team-of-engineers experience, and requirements elicitation must not be dressed up as team experience.
- **All 5 re-examined pre-checker drops upheld, none overturned** (Salesforce London FDE, Workato, Client Server Manchester, Hatched Recruitment, Brain Co.). Two carry corrections into the log. **Salesforce**: the drop is over-determined on 3+ yrs and the CS-degree gate, but must **not** lean on "prior customer-facing technical delivery experience ... a requirement, not a nice-to-have" — that is arguably the one clause MB Dev Studio's requirements→scoping→build→deploy line genuinely instantiates. **Hatched Recruitment**: flagged as a **single-clause drop** ("Experience in a Solutions Engineer, Sales Engineer or Technical Consultant role") rather than an over-determined one — everything else in it is reachable, so a sibling posting without that bullet should be re-opened.

**The word-level finding of this beat.** Gecko was kept and Brain Co. dropped on two pairs of words in otherwise near-identical Abu Dhabi "deployed engineer" postings: **"or relevant experience"** vs **"or a related field"**, and **"familiarity with data structures"** vs **"strong foundation in ... data structures and algorithms"**. Change either pair and the verdicts swap. This is the sharpest evidence yet that these postings must be read clause by clause rather than skimmed for stack overlap — and the Palantir "preferred" error is the same lesson in the opposite direction.

**Delivered: 1 job** — JOBSEARCH_332, Gecko Robotics. Not padded to the 3–5 cap; three of the last four beats have delivered one or zero. It is the **first Lane B keep in five beats**, and it arrived through the city-search route the last two beats built.

Lane A's junior-frontend seam in Pakistan produced **nothing new at all** this run: every Islamabad/Lahore/Karachi frontend result was a posting already judged, which is the first time that lane has gone completely dry. The corrective is procedural rather than a new rule — **quote the required-qualifications block verbatim into the rationale before writing the case for a keep**, so the drop condition has to be looked at rather than skipped past. Rules 4, 8 and 11 each already forbid what happened; none of them fired at draft time because the relevant sentence was never read aloud.

**Three lane findings, and the first one reverses a standing hypothesis.**

**The methodological finding of this beat, and it is a genuinely new failure mode rather than a repeat.** Beats 6, 7 and 8 all died on arguments *from absence* — the rare keyword finally appearing, role-shape vibes, the missing years gate. It died on the next thing along, which the checker named **partial-credit accumulation** — *"the belief that enough affirmatively-met bullets can outvote a conceded one. They cannot; rule 15's corollary makes any conceded graded clause dispositive regardless of how the rest scores."* The sharpest sentence, and the one that should govern the next beat: **"The scorecard is not an input to a judgment call; it *is* the judgment."** Beat 8's corrective worked exactly as designed — it produced an honest scorecard with four failures on it — and the draft then forwarded past its own result. **No rule change is proposed:** rule 15's corollary already covers this, and the failure was in not applying it, not in its absence. No PR was opened this beat.

**Four lane findings.**
1. **The first affirmative employer-level sponsorship statement in nine beats, and it is not n8n.** Revolut's graduate programme states it outright: *"open to international applicants ready to relocate to one of our tech hubs in **Poland, Portugal, Spain, the UAE, or the UK**. We'll support you with **visa assistance, travel costs, and your first month of accommodation**."* Two of those are relocation targets the CV names. The posting itself drops on two conjunctive gates (graduation-year window 2025–2027; a CS/maths/physics degree with "or a **similar field**", which is the Huxley/BRAIN form and not Gecko's "or **relevant experience**"), and rule 5 runs one way only — sponsorship never rescues a posting. But standing decision 14's corollary is exactly this case: the drop is about the posting, the sponsorship line is about the employer. **Revolut joins the standing watch list**, and the query to run is for its non-degree-gated reqs. Running tally of postings that state sponsorship: five, of which **four state the negative** (n8n London, CLERA, Cognizant, SCOR, Aristocrat) and one states yes.
2. **The London FDE market at top-tier AI employers is senior-gated end to end.** OpenAI's London ladder starts at *"5+ years of engineering or technical deployment experience"* (FDE) and *"7+ years of professional full stack engineering experience (excluding internships)"* (FDSE), then runs up through Manager and Technical Deployment Lead. There is no junior rung. Combined with beat 8's finding that the QA-automation seam is real but gated at 3–6 years, the picture is consistent: **the FDE title itself has no entry-level rung in the UK**, and the entry-level rungs that exist are at IBM (US-located, egress-blocked) and in graduate programmes that gate on a CS degree and a recent graduation year.
3. **The SThree rows are not jobs, and nine beats of log space have gone to them.** `_815` opens *"Please note the content of this advert does not represent a live vacancy"* and closes by saying a response just adds you to their database. The log now carries ten SThree rows across Manchester and London. They will keep filling the `solutions engineer implementation engineer` / Manchester slot every beat; that query's yield is effectively zero and Manchester may be worth rotating out or re-phrasing.
4. **The globally-remote seam cannot be reached by query text.** Beat 8 asked for an explicit `remote global` / `work from anywhere` phrasing; it was run and returned ordinary US-remote senior reqs. ElevenLabs' *"this role is remote and can be executed globally"* was found by reading a posting surfaced another way, and that is the only route to it — the clause lives in the JD body, not the location field.

**Lane A's thinning is now three beats deep and the shape has not changed.** `forward deployed engineer solutions engineer` / Islamabad → 0 for the third consecutive beat; `junior full stack developer TypeScript React Next.js` / Islamabad → 0 for the third; remote PK → 0 for the third. Lahore and Karachi still return volume and **every single result was already judged** — the Lahore query returned ten postings, all ten re-surfaces. Not one new Pakistan-based posting of any kind was surfaced this run.

**Delivered: 0 jobs.** The fifth consecutive empty beat, and correct under rule 8 — one posting was drafted on an affirmative argument for the first time in four beats, and it was still dropped, over-determined. Across nine beats the record is 1, 1, 0, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0.

Edisyl's Forward-Deployed AI Data Engineer reached its **eleventh ID across nine beats** (`_855`).

**Draft shortlist — 1, sent as an explicit borderline: JOBSEARCH_920, ICT Systems "AI Automation Engineer (n8n & AI Agent Developer)" (Karachi).** It was drafted, and flagged as an intended drop, because it is the densest affirmative overlap with shipped artifacts seen in several beats — n8n, TypeScript, Docker, Git, REST, JSON, MCP and Playwright all appear in its required or preferred lists.

**Checker (separate `general-purpose` subagent) — kept 0, dropped 1, upheld all 8 audited drops, no overturns.** It re-pulled all nine postings and verified every quoted requirement verbatim before ruling, and it improved two rationales rather than merely agreeing with them.

**The methodological finding of this beat, and it belongs to the checker.** The draft had scored "strong hands-on experience with n8n" as **MET** on the strength of the self-hosted n8n platform. The checker refused it: *"Installing the runtime is not building the workflows; there is no shipped n8n workflow or agent on the fact sheet at all."* The JD asks for someone who "has built production-ready AI agents **using n8n**"; what the CV evidences is deploying and operating the n8n **platform** — Docker on ARM64, Tailscale Funnel, a seven-phase runbook. Those are different skills that share a proper noun. The same correction landed on the MCP harness: it *exposes Playwright to agents*, i.e. it builds a tool an agent calls — it is not an autonomous agent. **This is a new and sharper form of rule 4 than any beat has recorded: the keyword hit can be inside a genuinely shipped artifact and still not be the thing the posting is asking for.** Beat 6 learned that a rare keyword cannot carry a scorecard; beat 10 learned that a rare keyword can be *mis-scored as met* by a rationale that names a real project. No rule change is proposed — rule 4 already says a keyword hit is not a match, and the fix is to state *which verb* the fact-sheet line satisfies, not to add a rule.

**Four lane findings.**
1. **Karachi is a live Lane A city and it has been under-queried for ten beats.** One query — `solutions engineer technical support engineer` — returned **ten results of which nine were new postings**, the highest new-posting ratio of any Lane A query in the log; Islamabad and Lahore between them produced **zero** new postings this run for the fourth beat running. Karachi is where the Pakistani market's junior and trainee seats actually are: this single query surfaced a Trainee DevOps Engineer, a Trainee Automation Sales Engineer, an IT Engineer Trainee and a Graduate Trainee Program alongside the n8n req. None of them cleared, but the *seam* is real and Islamabad does not have it. **Action for beat 11: Karachi joins the standing Lane A rotation, and the `solutions engineer technical support engineer` phrasing should be re-run against Islamabad and Lahore — it is the phrasing, not only the city, that opened this.**
2. **Beat 9's Revolut action is answered, and the answer is that the ladder is gated all the way down.** Both hubs queried; every graduate and internship req carries an unqualified enrolment or graduation-year gate ("**to be in your penultimate year** of a bachelor's or master's degree ... graduating in 2028" for the internship; "graduated in 2025, 2026, or 2027" for the graduate programme), and the non-graduate engineering reqs are Lead-level or data-science-shaped. The one req where her banking half is the relevant half — Payments Implementation Manager, Dubai — asks for **5+ years in local payment schemes** plus payment-system certifications. **Revolut's sponsorship offer is real and now confirmed on a second req family** ("Poland, Portugal, the UAE, or the UK ... visa assistance, travel costs, and accommodation") **but there is no rung on the Revolut ladder that a career-changer without a current degree enrolment can reach.** It stays on the watch list for lateral reqs only; the graduate/internship family should not be re-queried.
3. **Germany's first drop is a language gate, not a requirements gate — and that is new information about the market, not about the candidate.** KlickPiloten (`_893`) is the first posting in ten beats to **explicitly refuse a years bar**: "Uns interessiert, was du gebaut hast, nicht wie lange du dabei bist", with work samples in place of a cover letter. It asks for exactly the delivery-consulting shape the CV is positioned for. It requires **verhandlungssicheres Deutsch**. Standing decision 14 made Germany the highest-yield geography for a candidate who needs sponsorship; this beat qualifies that — **the German-market reqs that are open on experience tend to be client-facing consulting reqs, and client-facing consulting in Germany is conducted in German.** The German postings that would not need German are the product-engineering ones, which are the ones that gate on years. Worth watching whether an English-language German req with no years bar exists at all; n8n's own reqs (English-language, sponsorship-affirmative) are the place to look.
4. **The two closest near-misses of the run failed on the same missing block, which makes it a gap pattern rather than bad luck.** `_920` (ICT Systems) and `_893` (KlickPiloten) both died partly on **direct LLM-API practice** — "strong knowledge of OpenAI API, Claude API, Gemini API"; "Praxis mit LLM-APIs: Tool Use, strukturierte Ausgaben, Embeddings" — and `_920`, `_925`, `_854` and `_922` all died partly on **a datastore beyond Firestore** (SQL/PostgreSQL/MySQL, or vector DBs + RAG). The checker's summary: *"The n8n and MCP lines on her CV keep generating near-misses precisely because rule 15 forbids them from carrying a scorecard on their own."* **This is the sharpest evidence yet for what the pending flagship app should contain** — an LLM-API integration with tool use and structured outputs, over a real datastore — and it is recorded here as an observation, not pushed as a fact-sheet change.

**Delivered: 0 jobs.** The sixth consecutive empty beat, and correct under rule 8. Across ten beats the record is 1, 1, 0, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0. **No PR was opened this beat:** the two candidate rule changes both turned out to be already covered — the n8n mis-scoring by rule 4, and the Karachi/phrasing lesson by standing decision 14's existing "where a market is searched matters as much as how", which the Karachi result confirms rather than amends.

Because Indeed returns ten results per query and assigns IDs per session, *running the mandated query is not the same as covering the market it was meant to open*, and a single run of a query can silently miss the best posting in it. That posting turned out to be **the most on-target Lane A shape in several beats**: post-sale implementation delivery at a Constellation Software subsidiary, **no degree requirement, no years bar, and SQL demoted to nice-to-have** — the exact three gates that have killed nearly every Lane A candidate for ten beats, all three absent at once. But it drops **by judgement**, and it would otherwise have been invisible. **Action for beat 12: re-run a query axis a second time with a varied phrasing before concluding it is covered, and treat "ran the query" and "covered the seam" as different claims.**
